People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange, or unlikely. I do understand that.

It’s just hard because I came in really because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and morning announcements at school are sort of… coded messages about me, even when my colleagues gently show me they were sent to everyone. I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to be difficult or dramatic, honestly. It just feels very real at the time.

My colleagues have shown me they went to everyone, and they’ve been kind about it, but I still can’t quite shake the feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings about coded messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about four months, I think. Not constant, and I’ve tried to be sensible about it, but that’s roughly when I started feeling the messages might be aimed at me. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose I’m here because I’ve been getting a bit worried about how I’m interpreting things at school. I’m probably making too much of it, but sometimes staff emails or even the morning announcements feel like they’re… sort of coded messages about me. My colleagues have shown me they went to everyone, and they’ve been kind about it, but I still can’t quite shake the feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings about coded messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about four months, I think. Not constant, and I’ve tried to be sensible about it, but that’s roughly when I started feeling the messages might be aimed at me. There’s also been a voice I sometimes answer, around the same sort of time… I’m sorry, I know that sounds odd.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voice you sometimes answer, what does it say?\n\nPatient: It’s not… it’s not a long conversation, usually. More like a woman’s voice, quite clear, saying my name, or making a small comment about what I’m doing. It tends to happen when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied.\n\nSometimes I’ll answer automatically, just “yes?” or something, and then I realise there isn’t anyone there. I know that must sound strange. It’s not all the time, and I’m trying not to make too much of it, but it has unsettled me a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice or the coded messages are actually about you or intended for you in a way that doesn't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose that’s the part that’s been worrying me. I do know, logically, the emails were sent to everyone, and people have been very kind about showing me that. But there’s still this feeling I can’t quite get rid of, that certain staff emails or the morning announcements are somehow meant for me, like there’s a coded meaning in them about me.\n\nI’m not saying I’m certain, exactly… I know it probably doesn’t sound sensible. But it feels directed at me, even when other people would say it isn’t.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that these messages are actually about you?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely certain… I don’t want to sound as if I’m insisting on it. I can see, when my colleagues point it out, that the emails went to everyone, and the announcements are just normal school things.\n\nBut I’d say there’s still a part of me that feels quite sure in the moment — like they’re coded, or somehow about me specifically. Then afterwards I try to talk myself out of it. So… not 100%, but enough that I can’t seem to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that these messages are about you as something that seems strange or unlikely to most people?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange, or unlikely. I do understand that.\n\nIt’s just hard because I came in really because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and morning announcements at school are sort of… coded messages about me, even when my colleagues gently show me they were sent to everyone. I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to be difficult or dramatic, honestly. It just feels very real at the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that these messages are coded and about you is something that most people would consider to be a strange or unrealistic belief?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose most people would think it sounds strange or unrealistic. I do understand that, and I’m not trying to say everyone else is wrong.\n\nIt’s just that I came in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that certain staff emails and the morning announcements at school are really coded messages about me. Even when colleagues very kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this part of me that feels they’re somehow meant for me. I know that probably doesn’t sound very sensible.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the voice you hear as saying things that are unusual or don't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose they would sound unusual to other people. It’s not that it says long, bizarre things, or anything like that.\n\nSometimes when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied, I hear a woman’s voice say my name, or make a little comment about what I’m doing. It can be clear enough that I answer before I realize no one’s there.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t make much sense… I’m sorry, I’m not sure if I’m explaining it properly.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking that the messages are coded and about you, do you ever think that maybe you're wrong about that?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, sometimes. I mean, I try to tell myself, “They were sent to everyone, this can’t just be about me.” Especially when a colleague shows me the email list or explains the announcement.\n\nBut even then, the feeling doesn’t really go away properly. I can see there might be another explanation, but part of me still thinks there’s something coded in it, something meant for me. I know that probably sounds frustrating… I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about coded messages and the voice might be a sign of something not quite right with your thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it might be. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I did come in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and the morning announcements at school are coded messages about me.\n\nEven when my colleagues kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this feeling that they’re somehow meant for me. So… yes, I can see that might mean something isn’t quite right with my thinking. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. My colleagues have shown me they went to everyone, and they’ve been kind about it, but I still can’t quite shake the feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings about coded messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about four months, I think. Not constant, and I’ve tried to be sensible about it, but that’s roughly when I started feeling the messages might be aimed at me. There’s also been a voice I sometimes answer, around the same sort of time… I’m sorry, I know that sounds odd.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voice you sometimes answer, what does it say?\n\nPatient: It’s not… it’s not a long conversation, usually. More like a woman’s voice, quite clear, saying my name, or making a small comment about what I’m doing. It tends to happen when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied.\n\nSometimes I’ll answer automatically, just “yes?” or something, and then I realise there isn’t anyone there. I know that must sound strange. It’s not all the time, and I’m trying not to make too much of it, but it has unsettled me a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice or the coded messages are actually about you or intended for you in a way that doesn't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose that’s the part that’s been worrying me. I do know, logically, the emails were sent to everyone, and people have been very kind about showing me that. But there’s still this feeling I can’t quite get rid of, that certain staff emails or the morning announcements are somehow meant for me, like there’s a coded meaning in them about me.\n\nI’m not saying I’m certain, exactly… I know it probably doesn’t sound sensible. But it feels directed at me, even when other people would say it isn’t.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that these messages are actually about you?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely certain… I don’t want to sound as if I’m insisting on it. I can see, when my colleagues point it out, that the emails went to everyone, and the announcements are just normal school things.\n\nBut I’d say there’s still a part of me that feels quite sure in the moment — like they’re coded, or somehow about me specifically. Then afterwards I try to talk myself out of it. So… not 100%, but enough that I can’t seem to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that these messages are about you as something that seems strange or unlikely to most people?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange, or unlikely. I do understand that.\n\nIt’s just hard because I came in really because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and morning announcements at school are sort of… coded messages about me, even when my colleagues gently show me they were sent to everyone. I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to be difficult or dramatic, honestly. It just feels very real at the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that these messages are coded and about you is something that most people would consider to be a strange or unrealistic belief?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose most people would think it sounds strange or unrealistic. I do understand that, and I’m not trying to say everyone else is wrong.\n\nIt’s just that I came in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that certain staff emails and the morning announcements at school are really coded messages about me. Even when colleagues very kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this part of me that feels they’re somehow meant for me. I know that probably doesn’t sound very sensible.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the voice you hear as saying things that are unusual or don't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose they would sound unusual to other people. It’s not that it says long, bizarre things, or anything like that.\n\nSometimes when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied, I hear a woman’s voice say my name, or make a little comment about what I’m doing. It can be clear enough that I answer before I realize no one’s there.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t make much sense… I’m sorry, I’m not sure if I’m explaining it properly.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking that the messages are coded and about you, do you ever think that maybe you're wrong about that?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, sometimes. I mean, I try to tell myself, “They were sent to everyone, this can’t just be about me.” Especially when a colleague shows me the email list or explains the announcement.\n\nBut even then, the feeling doesn’t really go away properly. I can see there might be another explanation, but part of me still thinks there’s something coded in it, something meant for me. I know that probably sounds frustrating… I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about coded messages and the voice might be a sign of something not quite right with your thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it might be. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I did come in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and the morning announcements at school are coded messages about me.\n\nEven when my colleagues kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this feeling that they’re somehow meant for me. So… yes, I can see that might mean something isn’t quite right with my thinking. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people would find your belief about the coded messages to be unusual or unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they probably would. I mean, if someone else told me that ordinary staff emails or morning announcements were really coded messages about them, I suppose I’d think it sounded a bit unrealistic.\n\nI don’t want to seem difficult, because I do understand they’re sent to everyone. My colleagues have shown me that. But I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some part of them is meant for me. I know that may sound odd… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely certain… no. I do have doubts, especially when someone points out that the emails went to everyone, or that the announcement was just ordinary school business.\n\nBut the trouble is, even when I understand that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something in them meant for me. It’s not that I’m trying to be dramatic or unreasonable. It just feels… hard to let go of.\n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’m not sure they’re definitely true, but they feel true enough that it worries me. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I can see, when my colleagues point it out, that the emails went to everyone, and the announcements are just normal school things.\n\nBut I’d say there’s still a part of me that feels quite sure in the moment — like they’re coded, or somehow about me specifically. Then afterwards I try to talk myself out of it. So… not 100%, but enough that I can’t seem to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that these messages are about you as something that seems strange or unlikely to most people?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange, or unlikely. I do understand that.\n\nIt’s just hard because I came in really because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and morning announcements at school are sort of… coded messages about me, even when my colleagues gently show me they were sent to everyone. I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to be difficult or dramatic, honestly. It just feels very real at the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that these messages are coded and about you is something that most people would consider to be a strange or unrealistic belief?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose most people would think it sounds strange or unrealistic. I do understand that, and I’m not trying to say everyone else is wrong.\n\nIt’s just that I came in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that certain staff emails and the morning announcements at school are really coded messages about me. Even when colleagues very kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this part of me that feels they’re somehow meant for me. I know that probably doesn’t sound very sensible.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the voice you hear as saying things that are unusual or don't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose they would sound unusual to other people. It’s not that it says long, bizarre things, or anything like that.\n\nSometimes when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied, I hear a woman’s voice say my name, or make a little comment about what I’m doing. It can be clear enough that I answer before I realize no one’s there.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t make much sense… I’m sorry, I’m not sure if I’m explaining it properly.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking that the messages are coded and about you, do you ever think that maybe you're wrong about that?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, sometimes. I mean, I try to tell myself, “They were sent to everyone, this can’t just be about me.” Especially when a colleague shows me the email list or explains the announcement.\n\nBut even then, the feeling doesn’t really go away properly. I can see there might be another explanation, but part of me still thinks there’s something coded in it, something meant for me. I know that probably sounds frustrating… I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about coded messages and the voice might be a sign of something not quite right with your thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it might be. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I did come in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and the morning announcements at school are coded messages about me.\n\nEven when my colleagues kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this feeling that they’re somehow meant for me. So… yes, I can see that might mean something isn’t quite right with my thinking. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people would find your belief about the coded messages to be unusual or unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they probably would. I mean, if someone else told me that ordinary staff emails or morning announcements were really coded messages about them, I suppose I’d think it sounded a bit unrealistic.\n\nI don’t want to seem difficult, because I do understand they’re sent to everyone. My colleagues have shown me that. But I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some part of them is meant for me. I know that may sound odd… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely certain… no. I do have doubts, especially when someone points out that the emails went to everyone, or that the announcement was just ordinary school business.\n\nBut the trouble is, even when I understand that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something in them meant for me. It’s not that I’m trying to be dramatic or unreasonable. It just feels… hard to let go of.\n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’m not sure they’re definitely true, but they feel true enough that it worries me. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice could be completely wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose they could be completely wrong. I don’t like admitting that, because in the moment it really does feel as if those emails or announcements are somehow aimed at me.\n\nBut I know my colleagues have shown me they were sent to everyone, and I can see how, from the outside, it might just be me reading something into them that isn’t there. I’m trying to keep that possibility in mind.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Not all the time, no… I don’t want to make it sound as if I’m walking around convinced every minute.

I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to be difficult or dramatic, honestly. It just feels very real at the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that these messages are coded and about you is something that most people would consider to be a strange or unrealistic belief?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose most people would think it sounds strange or unrealistic. I do understand that, and I’m not trying to say everyone else is wrong.\n\nIt’s just that I came in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that certain staff emails and the morning announcements at school are really coded messages about me. Even when colleagues very kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this part of me that feels they’re somehow meant for me. I know that probably doesn’t sound very sensible.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the voice you hear as saying things that are unusual or don't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose they would sound unusual to other people. It’s not that it says long, bizarre things, or anything like that.\n\nSometimes when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied, I hear a woman’s voice say my name, or make a little comment about what I’m doing. It can be clear enough that I answer before I realize no one’s there.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t make much sense… I’m sorry, I’m not sure if I’m explaining it properly.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking that the messages are coded and about you, do you ever think that maybe you're wrong about that?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, sometimes. I mean, I try to tell myself, “They were sent to everyone, this can’t just be about me.” Especially when a colleague shows me the email list or explains the announcement.\n\nBut even then, the feeling doesn’t really go away properly. I can see there might be another explanation, but part of me still thinks there’s something coded in it, something meant for me. I know that probably sounds frustrating… I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about coded messages and the voice might be a sign of something not quite right with your thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it might be. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I did come in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and the morning announcements at school are coded messages about me.\n\nEven when my colleagues kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this feeling that they’re somehow meant for me. So… yes, I can see that might mean something isn’t quite right with my thinking. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people would find your belief about the coded messages to be unusual or unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they probably would. I mean, if someone else told me that ordinary staff emails or morning announcements were really coded messages about them, I suppose I’d think it sounded a bit unrealistic.\n\nI don’t want to seem difficult, because I do understand they’re sent to everyone. My colleagues have shown me that. But I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some part of them is meant for me. I know that may sound odd… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely certain… no. I do have doubts, especially when someone points out that the emails went to everyone, or that the announcement was just ordinary school business.\n\nBut the trouble is, even when I understand that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something in them meant for me. It’s not that I’m trying to be dramatic or unreasonable. It just feels… hard to let go of.\n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’m not sure they’re definitely true, but they feel true enough that it worries me. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice could be completely wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose they could be completely wrong. I don’t like admitting that, because in the moment it really does feel as if those emails or announcements are somehow aimed at me.\n\nBut I know my colleagues have shown me they were sent to everyone, and I can see how, from the outside, it might just be me reading something into them that isn’t there. I’m trying to keep that possibility in mind.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself completely convinced by the idea that the messages are coded and about you?\n\nPatient: Not all the time, no… I don’t want to make it sound as if I’m walking around convinced every minute.\n\nIt’s more that when a staff email comes through, or during the morning announcements, I can suddenly feel very sure there’s something in it about me. Then later, especially if someone explains it or I calm down a bit, I can see it may not be true.\n\nSo… maybe it comes in waves. In the moment it can feel quite convincing, but I do try to question it. Is that answering what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I suppose… yes, probably most people would find it a bit strange if I said it plainly.

But it feels directed at me, even when other people would say it isn’t.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that these messages are actually about you?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely certain… I don’t want to sound as if I’m insisting on it. I can see, when my colleagues point it out, that the emails went to everyone, and the announcements are just normal school things.\n\nBut I’d say there’s still a part of me that feels quite sure in the moment — like they’re coded, or somehow about me specifically. Then afterwards I try to talk myself out of it. So… not 100%, but enough that I can’t seem to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that these messages are about you as something that seems strange or unlikely to most people?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange, or unlikely. I do understand that.\n\nIt’s just hard because I came in really because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and morning announcements at school are sort of… coded messages about me, even when my colleagues gently show me they were sent to everyone. I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to be difficult or dramatic, honestly. It just feels very real at the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that these messages are coded and about you is something that most people would consider to be a strange or unrealistic belief?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose most people would think it sounds strange or unrealistic. I do understand that, and I’m not trying to say everyone else is wrong.\n\nIt’s just that I came in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that certain staff emails and the morning announcements at school are really coded messages about me. Even when colleagues very kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this part of me that feels they’re somehow meant for me. I know that probably doesn’t sound very sensible.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the voice you hear as saying things that are unusual or don't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose they would sound unusual to other people. It’s not that it says long, bizarre things, or anything like that.\n\nSometimes when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied, I hear a woman’s voice say my name, or make a little comment about what I’m doing. It can be clear enough that I answer before I realize no one’s there.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t make much sense… I’m sorry, I’m not sure if I’m explaining it properly.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking that the messages are coded and about you, do you ever think that maybe you're wrong about that?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, sometimes. I mean, I try to tell myself, “They were sent to everyone, this can’t just be about me.” Especially when a colleague shows me the email list or explains the announcement.\n\nBut even then, the feeling doesn’t really go away properly. I can see there might be another explanation, but part of me still thinks there’s something coded in it, something meant for me. I know that probably sounds frustrating… I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about coded messages and the voice might be a sign of something not quite right with your thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it might be. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I did come in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and the morning announcements at school are coded messages about me.\n\nEven when my colleagues kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this feeling that they’re somehow meant for me. So… yes, I can see that might mean something isn’t quite right with my thinking. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people would find your belief about the coded messages to be unusual or unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they probably would. I mean, if someone else told me that ordinary staff emails or morning announcements were really coded messages about them, I suppose I’d think it sounded a bit unrealistic.\n\nI don’t want to seem difficult, because I do understand they’re sent to everyone. My colleagues have shown me that. But I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some part of them is meant for me. I know that may sound odd… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely certain… no. I do have doubts, especially when someone points out that the emails went to everyone, or that the announcement was just ordinary school business.\n\nBut the trouble is, even when I understand that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something in them meant for me. It’s not that I’m trying to be dramatic or unreasonable. It just feels… hard to let go of.\n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’m not sure they’re definitely true, but they feel true enough that it worries me. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice could be completely wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose they could be completely wrong. I don’t like admitting that, because in the moment it really does feel as if those emails or announcements are somehow aimed at me.\n\nBut I know my colleagues have shown me they were sent to everyone, and I can see how, from the outside, it might just be me reading something into them that isn’t there. I’m trying to keep that possibility in mind.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself completely convinced by the idea that the messages are coded and about you?\n\nPatient: Not all the time, no… I don’t want to make it sound as if I’m walking around convinced every minute.\n\nIt’s more that when a staff email comes through, or during the morning announcements, I can suddenly feel very sure there’s something in it about me. Then later, especially if someone explains it or I calm down a bit, I can see it may not be true.\n\nSo… maybe it comes in waves. In the moment it can feel quite convincing, but I do try to question it. Is that answering what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the things the voice says as strange or unbelievable to most people?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, probably most people would find it a bit strange if I said it plainly.\n\nIt’s not usually long or dramatic, though. It can sound clear enough that I’ll answer before I remember there isn’t anyone there.\n\nI know that probably sounds unbelievable. I’m probably making too much of it, but sometimes staff emails or even the morning announcements feel like they’re… sort of coded messages about me. My colleagues have shown me they went to everyone, and they’ve been kind about it, but I still can’t quite shake the feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings about coded messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about four months, I think. Not constant, and I’ve tried to be sensible about it, but that’s roughly when I started feeling the messages might be aimed at me. There’s also been a voice I sometimes answer, around the same sort of time… I’m sorry, I know that sounds odd.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voice you sometimes answer, what does it say?\n\nPatient: It’s not… it’s not a long conversation, usually. More like a woman’s voice, quite clear, saying my name, or making a small comment about what I’m doing. It tends to happen when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied.\n\nSometimes I’ll answer automatically, just “yes?” or something, and then I realise there isn’t anyone there. I know that must sound strange. It’s not all the time, and I’m trying not to make too much of it, but it has unsettled me a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice or the coded messages are actually about you or intended for you in a way that doesn't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose that’s the part that’s been worrying me. I do know, logically, the emails were sent to everyone, and people have been very kind about showing me that. But there’s still this feeling I can’t quite get rid of, that certain staff emails or the morning announcements are somehow meant for me, like there’s a coded meaning in them about me.\n\nI’m not saying I’m certain, exactly… I know it probably doesn’t sound sensible. But it feels directed at me, even when other people would say it isn’t.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that these messages are actually about you?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely certain… I don’t want to sound as if I’m insisting on it. I can see, when my colleagues point it out, that the emails went to everyone, and the announcements are just normal school things.\n\nBut I’d say there’s still a part of me that feels quite sure in the moment — like they’re coded, or somehow about me specifically. Then afterwards I try to talk myself out of it. So… not 100%, but enough that I can’t seem to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that these messages are about you as something that seems strange or unlikely to most people?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange, or unlikely. I do understand that.\n\nIt’s just hard because I came in really because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and morning announcements at school are sort of… coded messages about me, even when my colleagues gently show me they were sent to everyone. I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to be difficult or dramatic, honestly. It just feels very real at the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that these messages are coded and about you is something that most people would consider to be a strange or unrealistic belief?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose most people would think it sounds strange or unrealistic. I do understand that, and I’m not trying to say everyone else is wrong.\n\nIt’s just that I came in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that certain staff emails and the morning announcements at school are really coded messages about me. Even when colleagues very kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this part of me that feels they’re somehow meant for me. I know that probably doesn’t sound very sensible.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the voice you hear as saying things that are unusual or don't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose they would sound unusual to other people. It’s not that it says long, bizarre things, or anything like that.\n\nSometimes when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied, I hear a woman’s voice say my name, or make a little comment about what I’m doing. It can be clear enough that I answer before I realize no one’s there.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t make much sense… I’m sorry, I’m not sure if I’m explaining it properly.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking that the messages are coded and about you, do you ever think that maybe you're wrong about that?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, sometimes. I mean, I try to tell myself, “They were sent to everyone, this can’t just be about me.” Especially when a colleague shows me the email list or explains the announcement.\n\nBut even then, the feeling doesn’t really go away properly. I can see there might be another explanation, but part of me still thinks there’s something coded in it, something meant for me. I know that probably sounds frustrating… I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about coded messages and the voice might be a sign of something not quite right with your thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it might be. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I did come in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and the morning announcements at school are coded messages about me.\n\nEven when my colleagues kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this feeling that they’re somehow meant for me. So… yes, I can see that might mean something isn’t quite right with my thinking. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people would find your belief about the coded messages to be unusual or unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they probably would. I mean, if someone else told me that ordinary staff emails or morning announcements were really coded messages about them, I suppose I’d think it sounded a bit unrealistic.\n\nI don’t want to seem difficult, because I do understand they’re sent to everyone. My colleagues have shown me that. But I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some part of them is meant for me. I know that may sound odd… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely certain… no. I do have doubts, especially when someone points out that the emails went to everyone, or that the announcement was just ordinary school business.\n\nBut the trouble is, even when I understand that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something in them meant for me. It’s not that I’m trying to be dramatic or unreasonable. It just feels… hard to let go of.\n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’m not sure they’re definitely true, but they feel true enough that it worries me. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice could be completely wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose they could be completely wrong. I don’t like admitting that, because in the moment it really does feel as if those emails or announcements are somehow aimed at me.\n\nBut I know my colleagues have shown me they were sent to everyone, and I can see how, from the outside, it might just be me reading something into them that isn’t there. I’m trying to keep that possibility in mind.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself completely convinced by the idea that the messages are coded and about you?\n\nPatient: Not all the time, no… I don’t want to make it sound as if I’m walking around convinced every minute.\n\nIt’s more that when a staff email comes through, or during the morning announcements, I can suddenly feel very sure there’s something in it about me. Then later, especially if someone explains it or I calm down a bit, I can see it may not be true.\n\nSo… maybe it comes in waves. In the moment it can feel quite convincing, but I do try to question it. Is that answering what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the things the voice says as strange or unbelievable to most people?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, probably most people would find it a bit strange if I said it plainly.\n\nIt’s not usually long or dramatic, though. It’s more like, when the house is very quiet, or after the classroom has emptied, I’ll hear a woman’s voice say my name, or make a small comment about what I’m doing. It can sound clear enough that I’ll answer before I remember there isn’t anyone there.\n\nI know that probably sounds unbelievable. I’m not trying to make it into something bigger than it is, but yes… I think most people would find it odd. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do you feel completely convinced that the messages are actually coded and about you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely convinced all the time… I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut when certain staff emails come through, or during the morning announcements, there are moments where it feels very hard to shake the idea that they’re about me somehow, like there’s a coded meaning in them. Even when colleagues gently point out that the email went to everyone, part of me still keeps turning it over.\n\nSo… not constantly, but often enough that it’s been bothering me and that’s why I came in. Is that the kind of frequency you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I can see there might be another explanation, but part of me still thinks there’s something coded in it, something meant for me. I know that probably sounds frustrating… I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about coded messages and the voice might be a sign of something not quite right with your thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it might be. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I did come in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and the morning announcements at school are coded messages about me.\n\nEven when my colleagues kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this feeling that they’re somehow meant for me. So… yes, I can see that might mean something isn’t quite right with my thinking. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people would find your belief about the coded messages to be unusual or unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they probably would. I mean, if someone else told me that ordinary staff emails or morning announcements were really coded messages about them, I suppose I’d think it sounded a bit unrealistic.\n\nI don’t want to seem difficult, because I do understand they’re sent to everyone. My colleagues have shown me that. But I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some part of them is meant for me. I know that may sound odd… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely certain… no. I do have doubts, especially when someone points out that the emails went to everyone, or that the announcement was just ordinary school business.\n\nBut the trouble is, even when I understand that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something in them meant for me. It’s not that I’m trying to be dramatic or unreasonable. It just feels… hard to let go of.\n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’m not sure they’re definitely true, but they feel true enough that it worries me. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice could be completely wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose they could be completely wrong. I don’t like admitting that, because in the moment it really does feel as if those emails or announcements are somehow aimed at me.\n\nBut I know my colleagues have shown me they were sent to everyone, and I can see how, from the outside, it might just be me reading something into them that isn’t there. I’m trying to keep that possibility in mind.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself completely convinced by the idea that the messages are coded and about you?\n\nPatient: Not all the time, no… I don’t want to make it sound as if I’m walking around convinced every minute.\n\nIt’s more that when a staff email comes through, or during the morning announcements, I can suddenly feel very sure there’s something in it about me. Then later, especially if someone explains it or I calm down a bit, I can see it may not be true.\n\nSo… maybe it comes in waves. My colleagues have shown me they went to everyone, and they’ve been kind about it, but I still can’t quite shake the feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings about coded messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about four months, I think. Not constant, and I’ve tried to be sensible about it, but that’s roughly when I started feeling the messages might be aimed at me. There’s also been a voice I sometimes answer, around the same sort of time… I’m sorry, I know that sounds odd.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voice you sometimes answer, what does it say?\n\nPatient: It’s not… it’s not a long conversation, usually. More like a woman’s voice, quite clear, saying my name, or making a small comment about what I’m doing. It tends to happen when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied.\n\nSometimes I’ll answer automatically, just “yes?” or something, and then I realise there isn’t anyone there. I know that must sound strange. It’s not all the time, and I’m trying not to make too much of it, but it has unsettled me a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice or the coded messages are actually about you or intended for you in a way that doesn't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose that’s the part that’s been worrying me. I do know, logically, the emails were sent to everyone, and people have been very kind about showing me that. But there’s still this feeling I can’t quite get rid of, that certain staff emails or the morning announcements are somehow meant for me, like there’s a coded meaning in them about me.\n\nI’m not saying I’m certain, exactly… I know it probably doesn’t sound sensible. But it feels directed at me, even when other people would say it isn’t.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that these messages are actually about you?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely certain… I don’t want to sound as if I’m insisting on it. I can see, when my colleagues point it out, that the emails went to everyone, and the announcements are just normal school things.\n\nBut I’d say there’s still a part of me that feels quite sure in the moment — like they’re coded, or somehow about me specifically. Then afterwards I try to talk myself out of it. So… not 100%, but enough that I can’t seem to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that these messages are about you as something that seems strange or unlikely to most people?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange, or unlikely. I do understand that.\n\nIt’s just hard because I came in really because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and morning announcements at school are sort of… coded messages about me, even when my colleagues gently show me they were sent to everyone. I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to be difficult or dramatic, honestly. It just feels very real at the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that these messages are coded and about you is something that most people would consider to be a strange or unrealistic belief?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose most people would think it sounds strange or unrealistic. I do understand that, and I’m not trying to say everyone else is wrong.\n\nIt’s just that I came in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that certain staff emails and the morning announcements at school are really coded messages about me. Even when colleagues very kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this part of me that feels they’re somehow meant for me. I know that probably doesn’t sound very sensible.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the voice you hear as saying things that are unusual or don't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose they would sound unusual to other people. It’s not that it says long, bizarre things, or anything like that.\n\nSometimes when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied, I hear a woman’s voice say my name, or make a little comment about what I’m doing. It can be clear enough that I answer before I realize no one’s there.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t make much sense… I’m sorry, I’m not sure if I’m explaining it properly.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking that the messages are coded and about you, do you ever think that maybe you're wrong about that?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, sometimes. I mean, I try to tell myself, “They were sent to everyone, this can’t just be about me.” Especially when a colleague shows me the email list or explains the announcement.\n\nBut even then, the feeling doesn’t really go away properly. I can see there might be another explanation, but part of me still thinks there’s something coded in it, something meant for me. I know that probably sounds frustrating… I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about coded messages and the voice might be a sign of something not quite right with your thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it might be. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I did come in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and the morning announcements at school are coded messages about me.\n\nEven when my colleagues kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this feeling that they’re somehow meant for me. So… yes, I can see that might mean something isn’t quite right with my thinking. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people would find your belief about the coded messages to be unusual or unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they probably would. I mean, if someone else told me that ordinary staff emails or morning announcements were really coded messages about them, I suppose I’d think it sounded a bit unrealistic.\n\nI don’t want to seem difficult, because I do understand they’re sent to everyone. My colleagues have shown me that. But I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some part of them is meant for me. I know that may sound odd… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely certain… no. I do have doubts, especially when someone points out that the emails went to everyone, or that the announcement was just ordinary school business.\n\nBut the trouble is, even when I understand that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something in them meant for me. It’s not that I’m trying to be dramatic or unreasonable. It just feels… hard to let go of.\n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’m not sure they’re definitely true, but they feel true enough that it worries me. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice could be completely wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose they could be completely wrong. I don’t like admitting that, because in the moment it really does feel as if those emails or announcements are somehow aimed at me.\n\nBut I know my colleagues have shown me they were sent to everyone, and I can see how, from the outside, it might just be me reading something into them that isn’t there. I’m trying to keep that possibility in mind.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself completely convinced by the idea that the messages are coded and about you?\n\nPatient: Not all the time, no… I don’t want to make it sound as if I’m walking around convinced every minute.\n\nIt’s more that when a staff email comes through, or during the morning announcements, I can suddenly feel very sure there’s something in it about me. Then later, especially if someone explains it or I calm down a bit, I can see it may not be true.\n\nSo… maybe it comes in waves. In the moment it can feel quite convincing, but I do try to question it. Is that answering what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the things the voice says as strange or unbelievable to most people?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, probably most people would find it a bit strange if I said it plainly.\n\nIt’s not usually long or dramatic, though. It’s more like, when the house is very quiet, or after the classroom has emptied, I’ll hear a woman’s voice say my name, or make a small comment about what I’m doing. It can sound clear enough that I’ll answer before I remember there isn’t anyone there.\n\nI know that probably sounds unbelievable. I’m not trying to make it into something bigger than it is, but yes… I think most people would find it odd. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do you feel completely convinced that the messages are actually coded and about you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely convinced all the time… I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut when certain staff emails come through, or during the morning announcements, there are moments where it feels very hard to shake the idea that they’re about me somehow, like there’s a coded meaning in them. Even when colleagues gently point out that the email went to everyone, part of me still keeps turning it over.\n\nSo… not constantly, but often enough that it’s been bothering me and that’s why I came in. Is that the kind of frequency you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself doubting that the messages are coded and about you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do doubt it sometimes. I mean, I know the staff emails are sent to everyone, and my colleagues have very kindly pointed that out to me.\n\nIt’s just that even then, I can’t always shake the feeling that there’s something in the wording, or the timing of the announcement, that’s meant for me somehow. I do try to tell myself I may be reading too much into it.\n\nSo yes, I doubt it… but the feeling still comes back. Is that clear enough?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been doubting that the messages are coded and about you?\n\nPatient: I think… about four months, roughly. It’s a little hard to pin down exactly because at first I tried to brush it off and tell myself I was just tired or overthinking things.\n\nBut the doubts about the messages being coded, and the times I’ve caught myself responding to that voice… they’ve been going on for around four months now. I hope that’s the sort of timeframe you’re asking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that anyone else shares your belief that the messages are coded and about you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think anyone else really shares it. My colleagues have actually been quite kind about it and pointed out that the emails go to everyone, or that the announcements are just general school things.\n\nI suppose that’s part of why I came in — because even when they show me that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that certain wording or timing is somehow about me. But I don’t think others believe that with me, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

It’s a little hard to pin down exactly because at first I tried to brush it off and tell myself I was just tired or overthinking things.\n\nBut the doubts about the messages being coded, and the times I’ve caught myself responding to that voice… they’ve been going on for around four months now. I hope that’s the sort of timeframe you’re asking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that anyone else shares your belief that the messages are coded and about you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think anyone else really shares it. My colleagues have actually been quite kind about it and pointed out that the emails go to everyone, or that the announcements are just general school things.\n\nI suppose that’s part of why I came in — because even when they show me that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that certain wording or timing is somehow about me. But I don’t think others believe that with me, if that makes sense.\n\n[Last Question]\nFor how long have you been completely convinced that the messages are coded and about you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 56 / secondary_school_teacher / graduate\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\nmanifestation: \"The coded-message worries and the voice I sometimes answer have been happening for about four months.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I’d say I’ve been completely convinced the whole time… there have been moments where it felt very real, and then other times I’ve tried to talk myself out of it.

But the worries about the messages being coded, and the voice I sometimes answer, have been going on for about four months. I hope that answers what you mean.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose I’m here because I’ve been getting a bit worried about how I’m interpreting things at school. I’m probably making too much of it, but sometimes staff emails or even the morning announcements feel like they’re… sort of coded messages about me. My colleagues have shown me they went to everyone, and they’ve been kind about it, but I still can’t quite shake the feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings about coded messages?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about four months, I think. Not constant, and I’ve tried to be sensible about it, but that’s roughly when I started feeling the messages might be aimed at me. There’s also been a voice I sometimes answer, around the same sort of time… I’m sorry, I know that sounds odd.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voice you sometimes answer, what does it say?\n\nPatient: It’s not… it’s not a long conversation, usually. More like a woman’s voice, quite clear, saying my name, or making a small comment about what I’m doing. It tends to happen when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied.\n\nSometimes I’ll answer automatically, just “yes?” or something, and then I realise there isn’t anyone there. I know that must sound strange. It’s not all the time, and I’m trying not to make too much of it, but it has unsettled me a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice or the coded messages are actually about you or intended for you in a way that doesn't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose that’s the part that’s been worrying me. I do know, logically, the emails were sent to everyone, and people have been very kind about showing me that. But there’s still this feeling I can’t quite get rid of, that certain staff emails or the morning announcements are somehow meant for me, like there’s a coded meaning in them about me.\n\nI’m not saying I’m certain, exactly… I know it probably doesn’t sound sensible. But it feels directed at me, even when other people would say it isn’t.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that these messages are actually about you?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely certain… I don’t want to sound as if I’m insisting on it. I can see, when my colleagues point it out, that the emails went to everyone, and the announcements are just normal school things.\n\nBut I’d say there’s still a part of me that feels quite sure in the moment — like they’re coded, or somehow about me specifically. Then afterwards I try to talk myself out of it. So… not 100%, but enough that I can’t seem to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that these messages are about you as something that seems strange or unlikely to most people?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think most people would probably find it strange, or unlikely. I do understand that.\n\nIt’s just hard because I came in really because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and morning announcements at school are sort of… coded messages about me, even when my colleagues gently show me they were sent to everyone. I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to be difficult or dramatic, honestly. It just feels very real at the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that these messages are coded and about you is something that most people would consider to be a strange or unrealistic belief?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose most people would think it sounds strange or unrealistic. I do understand that, and I’m not trying to say everyone else is wrong.\n\nIt’s just that I came in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that certain staff emails and the morning announcements at school are really coded messages about me. Even when colleagues very kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this part of me that feels they’re somehow meant for me. I know that probably doesn’t sound very sensible.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the voice you hear as saying things that are unusual or don't make sense to other people?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I suppose they would sound unusual to other people. It’s not that it says long, bizarre things, or anything like that.\n\nSometimes when the house is quiet, or after the classroom has emptied, I hear a woman’s voice say my name, or make a little comment about what I’m doing. It can be clear enough that I answer before I realize no one’s there.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t make much sense… I’m sorry, I’m not sure if I’m explaining it properly.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking that the messages are coded and about you, do you ever think that maybe you're wrong about that?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, sometimes. I mean, I try to tell myself, “They were sent to everyone, this can’t just be about me.” Especially when a colleague shows me the email list or explains the announcement.\n\nBut even then, the feeling doesn’t really go away properly. I can see there might be another explanation, but part of me still thinks there’s something coded in it, something meant for me. I know that probably sounds frustrating… I’m trying to be reasonable about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about coded messages and the voice might be a sign of something not quite right with your thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it might be. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I did come in because I can’t seem to shake the feeling that some of the staff emails and the morning announcements at school are coded messages about me.\n\nEven when my colleagues kindly show me they were sent to everyone, there’s still this feeling that they’re somehow meant for me. So… yes, I can see that might mean something isn’t quite right with my thinking. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people would find your belief about the coded messages to be unusual or unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they probably would. I mean, if someone else told me that ordinary staff emails or morning announcements were really coded messages about them, I suppose I’d think it sounded a bit unrealistic.\n\nI don’t want to seem difficult, because I do understand they’re sent to everyone. My colleagues have shown me that. But I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some part of them is meant for me. I know that may sound odd… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely certain… no. I do have doubts, especially when someone points out that the emails went to everyone, or that the announcement was just ordinary school business.\n\nBut the trouble is, even when I understand that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that there’s something in them meant for me. It’s not that I’m trying to be dramatic or unreasonable. It just feels… hard to let go of.\n\nSo I suppose I’d say I’m not sure they’re definitely true, but they feel true enough that it worries me. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the coded messages and the voice could be completely wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose they could be completely wrong. I don’t like admitting that, because in the moment it really does feel as if those emails or announcements are somehow aimed at me.\n\nBut I know my colleagues have shown me they were sent to everyone, and I can see how, from the outside, it might just be me reading something into them that isn’t there. I’m trying to keep that possibility in mind.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself completely convinced by the idea that the messages are coded and about you?\n\nPatient: Not all the time, no… I don’t want to make it sound as if I’m walking around convinced every minute.\n\nIt’s more that when a staff email comes through, or during the morning announcements, I can suddenly feel very sure there’s something in it about me. Then later, especially if someone explains it or I calm down a bit, I can see it may not be true.\n\nSo… maybe it comes in waves. In the moment it can feel quite convincing, but I do try to question it. Is that answering what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the things the voice says as strange or unbelievable to most people?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, probably most people would find it a bit strange if I said it plainly.\n\nIt’s not usually long or dramatic, though. It’s more like, when the house is very quiet, or after the classroom has emptied, I’ll hear a woman’s voice say my name, or make a small comment about what I’m doing. It can sound clear enough that I’ll answer before I remember there isn’t anyone there.\n\nI know that probably sounds unbelievable. I’m not trying to make it into something bigger than it is, but yes… I think most people would find it odd. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do you feel completely convinced that the messages are actually coded and about you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely convinced all the time… I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut when certain staff emails come through, or during the morning announcements, there are moments where it feels very hard to shake the idea that they’re about me somehow, like there’s a coded meaning in them. Is that the kind of frequency you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself doubting that the messages are coded and about you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do doubt it sometimes. I mean, I know the staff emails are sent to everyone, and my colleagues have very kindly pointed that out to me.\n\nIt’s just that even then, I can’t always shake the feeling that there’s something in the wording, or the timing of the announcement, that’s meant for me somehow. I do try to tell myself I may be reading too much into it.\n\nSo yes, I doubt it… but the feeling still comes back. Is that clear enough?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been doubting that the messages are coded and about you?\n\nPatient: I think… about four months, roughly.
